design, or appropriation and inversion; record mixed cases rather than forcing a
single label. Compare outcomes across recipient architectures and institutional
settings. Ask what the constructor knew, should reasonably have foreseen,
disclosed, tested, and changed after adverse offspring became visible.

A mythology must be revised when its means no longer produce its stated ends. It must be retired when correction cannot restore reality coupling without preserving a destructive attractor. Retirement should be treated as guided unbraiding and rebraiding, not abandonment into a semantic vacuum.

### 36. Evaluation criteria

Engagement, recall, and agreement are insufficient measures. Evaluation should span the complete causal chain.

| Evaluation level | Example questions |
|---|---|
| Reception | Was the mythology understood, remembered, and experienced as recognition rather than alien imposition? |
| Recipient reconstruction | What factual, causal, moral, and authorization model did recipients actually reconstruct; which omissions, cues, and assumed codebook produced it? |
| Relevant-structure appraisal | Which structures did recipients experience as morally important; what support or damage did they infer; and did actual effects match that representation across time, persons, and dimensions? |
| Internalization | Can participants state reasons, modify the account, and act without constant surveillance? |
| Psychological gradient | Which immediate rewards and avoidance patterns sustain participation? |
| Drive direction | Do repeated actions build capability, relationship, creation, maintenance, and posterity? |
| Sublimation architecture | Which drives are transformed into which capacities; which mythologies, practices, systems, and institutions recurrently perform and transmit that work? |
| Lifted sublimation generator | Which installed meta-rules produce binding directions, what actuates them, and through which practices do they become durable character, capability, relationship, or institution? |
| Generator valence | Is the generator merely effective, or are its outputs life-supporting, person-preserving, capability-producing, reality-answerable, and corrigible? |
| Generative centrality | Which downstream relationships and institutions depend upon those capacities, how replaceable is the architecture, and does felt relevance track the dependency? |
| Humanity boundary | Does the mythology preserve complete equal standing while presenting plural human possibility, or convert Humanity into a collective subject that consumes concrete persons? |
| Aspirational exemplars | Do *Leuchttürme* and near exemplars make valued possibilities visible and practicable without grading human standing or acquiring authority outside their domain? |
| Reflective audit | Can recipients identify a binding verdict's rule, provenance, framing, jurisdiction, and affective signature, and can evidence or consequence actually revise it? |
| Symbolic activation and reprogramming | Which carriers activate which installed classifications; is an observed change temporary activation, repeated reinforcement, or durable lens reprogramming? |
| Role enactment | Are participants doing the reality-facing work or mainly displaying identity? |
| Institutional embodiment | Do rules, rewards, appointments, rituals, and budgets tell the same story? |
| Coordination | Can differentiated actors contribute without forced uniformity? |
| Coupling range | Does cooperation cross the groups required for the task? |
| Epistemic integrity | Can evidence, dissent, and failure revise the mythology? |
| Material consequence | What happens to health, safety, production, trust, ecology, infrastructure, and prosperity? |
| Trajectory and destination | How does the observed path differ from the diagnosed baseline and the declared \(D^*\); which receivers, institutions, rivals, and consequences account for the difference? |
| Doctrinal progeny | Which descendants recur; are they related by entailment, affordance, selection, concealed causal design, or appropriation; and what does the complete distribution show? |
| Receiver architecture | Does the same doctrine support CPU-centred rule commitment, first-order recapture, or non-uptake under different affective, reflective, and institutional conditions? |
| Wrapper–implementation integrity | Were the bigOther-facing and institutional mechanisms, foreseeable effects, and material trade-offs disclosed alongside the CPU-facing doctrine? |
| Exit and recovery | Can a participant leave, change mind, repair harm, and retain ordinary dignity? |
| Braid integrity | Do its mutually supporting strands create durability without concealing contradiction or error? |
| Apologetic corrigibility | Can it defend its goods, answer strong objections, and repair false strands? |
| Polemical precision | Can it contest a failed rival without dissolving sound knowledge, ordinary trust, or the possibility of replacement? |
| Actor–slot fit | Do the dispositions, competence, credibility, and incentives of the selected actors fit the function they perform? |
| Authorization integrity | Is authority genuinely recognized through the relevant solidarity order, or merely asserted, captured, or simulated? |
| Authorization payload | What power, jurisdiction, duration, discretion, oversight, revocability, termination rule, and epistemic spillover follow from accepting the mythology? |
| *Herrschaftsideologie* | Does claimed privileged access to truth, morality, history, or collective interest become authority over persons who allegedly cannot see for themselves? |
| Institutional continuity | Can the function survive succession while remaining corrigible and faithful to its purpose? |
| Adversarial resilience | Can the mythology resist dark arts without adopting them? |
| Intergenerational consequence | What dispositions, institutions, debts, skills, and environments are handed forward? |

Several metrics require long horizons. A mythology can generate immediate unity by consuming trust, savings, infrastructure, fertility, reputation, or institutional independence accumulated by previous generations. Short-term mobilization should therefore be evaluated alongside changes in inherited capital.

A practical scorecard should preserve separate measures rather than collapse them into one number. High resonance cannot compensate automatically for destructive consequence; high material output cannot compensate automatically for systemic lying or coercive capture.

### 37. Testable hypotheses and research programme

The framework is intended to generate falsifiable research rather than explain every outcome after the fact.

| Hypothesis | Prediction | Possible test |
|---|---|---|
| Universal activation | Some mythological structures activate species-typical mechanisms across populations. | Cross-cultural experiments using common narrative and moral stimuli. |
| Dispositional modulation | Different dispositional configurations respond differently to the same mythology. | Pre-measured traits interacted with randomized narrative exposure. |
| Resonance interaction | Matched mythology produces greater transportation, retention, identification, and enactment than unmatched mythology. | Preregistered factorial experiments comparing matched and mismatched frames and roles. |
| Relevant-structure resonance | Independently measured relevance interacts with represented support or damage to predict moral appraisal, mythology adoption, and action readiness. | Measure relevance before exposure; randomize credible causal accounts of a mythology's effects on the same structures; compare appraisal and adoption while measuring actual-effect beliefs separately. |
| Change-indexed moral mobilization | A condition represented as active continuation, prevented transition, or culpable omission produces more intense moral appraisal and action readiness than the same material condition represented statically. | Randomize static, active-continuation, prevented-alternative, and omission descriptions while holding facts constant; test interaction with independently measured relevance. |
| Mirrored relevance | Identification with a represented person makes that person's relationships, injuries, responsibilities, or future more morally relevant to the recipient. | Randomize perspective and identification cues; measure immediate appraisal, sacrifice choices, resource allocation, and generalization beyond the story. |
| Foregrounded relevance | Selective presentation of affected structures changes present salience and moral appraisal without necessarily changing durable relevance rankings. | Compare one-shot salience with delayed rankings and behaviour; repeat exposure to distinguish activation from reprogramming. |
| Audience-modelled reconstruction | The same compressed message produces different factual, causal, moral, and authorization models in audiences with different installed codebooks, and a message designed for one audience more reliably produces the model anticipated for it. | Elicit full recipient reconstructions after crossed message-by-audience exposure; compare literal content, predicted model, actual model, and expanded controls. |
| Sublimation-carrier activation | A culturally familiar carrier activates a richer and more conduct-directing complex of sublimation-related classifications in recipients whose lenses contain the associated programming than in mismatched recipients. | Cross carriers and audiences; measure reconstructed meanings, sentiment classifications, action readiness, costly practice, and persistence while distinguishing familiarity from agreement. |
| Counter-mythological reprogramming | Repeated rival reclassification combined with supporting rewards, sanctions, systems, and exemplars changes appraisal and practice beyond the temporary salience produced by one exposure. | Compare one-shot activation with longitudinal, institutionally reinforced exposure; measure durable lens mappings, Beseelung, conduct, transmission, and recovery after the signal is removed. |
| Generative-centrality/relevance mismatch | Sublimation architectures upon which many downstream capabilities depend are more vulnerable to under-maintenance when their generative centrality is high but their felt relevance is low or negative. | Measure relevance independently from capability production and dependency; compare investment, transmission, replacement availability, inherited-capital consumption, and delayed institutional outcomes across cases. |
| Duty-bootstrap | Acquiring a capacity for rule-bound self-command supports later durable character and capability even where immediate inclination changes. | Longitudinally compare matched formation regimes while measuring delayed gratification, rule transfer across contexts, self-command, capability development, and whether effects depend on surveillance or sanction. |
| Reflective-audit capability | Generators that obligate recipients to inspect rule, framing, authority, evidence, and consequence remain more corrigible than generators that reward only correct output and loyalty. | Compare curricula or institutions with matched content but different audit practices; measure transfer, symmetric application, response to counterevidence, authority correction, and ceremonial rationalization. |
| Aspirational-exemplar activation | Credible exemplars raise aspiration and persistence most reliably when they make a valued possibility identity-compatible and are joined to attainable strategies and near exemplars. | Randomize distant exemplars, near exemplars, pathway information, and domain fit; measure possible selves, practice, persistence, performance, worship, envy, and discouragement. |
| Humanity two-message effect | Combining unconditional standing with an open horizon of development supports aspiration without making worth conditional on achievement. | Compare standing-only, achievement-only, combined, and neutral framings; measure dignity attribution under weakness or dissent, aspiration, effort, sacrifice authorization, and perfectionistic distress. |
| Protector-framing authorization | Threatened-structure and protector framing increases willingness to delegate power beyond any increase in agreement with the underlying proposal. | Hold policy content constant; vary threat, culprit, and protector roles; measure agreement separately from discretion, duration, oversight, opponent restriction, and jurisdictional spillover. |
| Cognitive-relief pathway | Under complexity, uncertainty, public exposure, or responsibility, an authority-endorsed compressed formula gains an adoption advantage partly through subjective relief and reduced checking. | Vary load, time pressure, publicity, authority endorsement, and cost of a check; test relief as mediator and willingness to inspect qualifications as an outcome. |
| Age amplification | Receptivity differences associated with dispositions increase as people select congruent niches. | Longitudinal studies of traits, environments, mythology exposure, and response. |
| Polygenic prediction | Future polygenic scores predict some differential mythology receptivity. | Prospective genomic studies with out-of-sample prediction and strict population controls. |
| Incremental prediction | Polygenic scores add prediction beyond demographics and self-report. | Nested models evaluated on held-out samples. |
| Cultural-niche reinforcement | Adoption leads people to select relationships and institutions that reinforce the mythology. | Longitudinal network and migration studies after conversion or identity change. |
| Cultural-meta-environment | Distributed mythological and institutional environments create systematic non-genetic formation not captured by the shared household. | Designs separating household, peer-network, institutional, media, regional, and cohort exposure while modelling genetic similarity. |
| Gene–culture feedback | Durable mythologies alter status, partner choice, fertility, migration, or survival in ways that change selection pressures. | Historical-demographic and genomic analysis with rival causal models. |
| Fast evolution | Persistent cultural selection pressures produce detectable dispositional change over historical time. | Ancient and modern genomic comparisons linked to documented changes in marriage, status, fertility, migration, or survival. |
| Population-history | Historical gene–culture trajectories predict different response distributions across societies. | Multi-population replications with measurement-invariance and ancestry-sensitive designs. |
| Producer–guardian | Producer-like and guardian-like configurations form partially stable, possibly heritable clusters with different mythology preferences. | Latent-profile, twin, genomic, and experimental-receptivity studies. |
| Marriage-mediated coupling range | Weaker intensive kinship predicts wider non-kin trust and greater resonance of impersonal civic mythology. | Kinship-network, institutional, historical, and cross-cultural studies. |
| Lifted-solidarity compatibility | Democracy is more durable where political loss remains compatible with shared membership and impersonal law. | Comparative institutional studies measuring civic meta-solidarity rather than formal rules alone. |
| Genetic-hardware fit | Dispositional or polygenic differences predict the cost and durability of sustaining impersonal office, loyal opposition, and abstract civic reciprocity. | Matched institutional comparisons combined with longitudinal dispositional and genomic measures. |
| Cultural interoperability breakdown (Babelization) | Declining effective meta-order thickness or span, combined with rising local coherence and intergroup semantic decoupling, predicts society-wide coordination failure below the anti-Babel floor. | Longitudinal language, network, trust, institutional-legitimacy, lifted-solidarity, and collective-action measures; test linear, threshold, bottleneck, and saturation models. |
| Residual interoperability scale | As wider interoperability fails, the characteristic size of internally interoperable islands may contract from mass domains through subcultures and very small groups toward the individual limit. | Joint semantic-network, relationship-network, trust, procedure-legitimacy, and group-membership measures across nested social scales and domains. |
| Loneliness as an interoperability consequence | A shrinking residual scale increases loneliness especially where contact remains abundant but mutual recognition, translation, repair, and stable micro-group membership repeatedly fail. | Separate subjective loneliness from objective isolation; measure translation cost, felt recognition, semantic fit, repair success, group stability, contact volume, and the demographic, material, technological, and clinical alternatives. |
| Culture-mixing threshold | Collective agency declines when incompatible first-order mythologies multiply faster than credible second-order integration. | Longitudinal comparison of migration or subcultural fragmentation rates, semantic overlap, civic identity, and coordination outcomes. |
| Directed guardian empathy | Care persists more strongly for a hostile actor classified as oppressed than for an equivalent hostile actor classified as dominant. | Factorial experiments independently varying harm, hostility, group power, and ideological identity. |
| Carrier–replicator divergence | Some mythologies increase their own transmission through costly carrier behaviour even while reducing carrier capability or survival. | Longitudinal studies measuring transmission against health, capability, reciprocity, fertility, and exit costs. |
| Downhill capture | Immediate moral and social rewards predict retention even when promised outcomes repeatedly fail. | Experiments and longitudinal studies separating outcome satisfaction from ritualized repetition. |
| Constructive attractor | Belonging and status attached to real mastery outperform debunking alone in durable exit from destructive mythology. | Randomized or quasi-experimental recovery and prevention programmes. |
| Reality coupling | Mythologies attached to observable adaptive practices correct more effectively than symbolically insulated mythologies. | Comparative field studies of institutions with different feedback structures. |
| Braided redundancy | Mythologies whose central meanings are supported by compatible story, role, ritual, and institution survive loss of any single expression better than slogan-centred campaigns. | Network analysis and controlled removal of communication or institutional supports. |
| Polemical vacancy | Negative delegitimation without an embodied successor increases generalized cynicism, anomie, or capture by a third mythology. | Longitudinal studies of institutional trust and identity following successful negative campaigns. |
| Critique–construction asymmetry | Where negative operations are more specified, repeatable, and rewarded than successor construction and maintenance, achieved settlements are more often recoded as renewed domination and mobilization persists after the original demand is substantially met. | Longitudinally compare the operational detail, institutional investment, status rewards, positive practices, response to achieved demands, successor grievances, and stopping rules of matched programmes. |
| Controlled replacement | Preserving sound strands and transition roles during unbraiding produces more stable adoption than total repudiation of the incumbent. | Comparative historical studies and field experiments on organizational or civic reform. |
| Systemic-slot selection | People who become recognized apologetic or polemical actors display relevant dispositional and network characteristics before formal appointment or public recognition. | Prospective studies of candidates, trajectories into roles, personality, status networks, and later recognition. |
| Recognition premium | The same argument has greater cultural effect when voiced by an actor ratified by the relevant solidarity order than by a self-appointed claimant. | Experiments varying identical messages by speaker, office, institutional endorsement, and audience identity. |
| Authority scaling | First-order solidarity concentrates apologetic and polemical authority more strongly in embodied persons, whereas lifted solidarity locates more of it in persistent offices and institutions. | Comparative studies measuring person-dependence, office continuity, succession, and response to actor replacement. |
| Institutional-actor persistence | Where the institution is the effective actor, recognizable apologetic or polemical policy persists across turnover of individual representatives. | Interrupted time-series and succession studies of courts, churches, parties, professions, universities, and media organizations. |
| Pathological selection | Death-driven or self-protective systems preferentially select actors whose dispositions support rationalization, persecution, closure, or institutional survival over stated purpose. | Longitudinal selection studies comparing recruitment, promotion, dissent tolerance, and consequence-bearing across institutions. |
| Offspring-profile recurrence | A doctrine with a stable generative spirit produces similar authority, subjecthood, and correction patterns across partially independent receivers and settings more often than rival explanations predict. | Code descendant cases blind to doctrine identity; distinguish shared templates and geopolitical pressures from independent recurrence; compare against matched doctrines and settings. |
| Receiver-architecture bifurcation | The same abstract doctrine is more likely to undergo first-order recapture where affect settles on current outputs and interpreters, and more likely to remain CPU-centred where reflective capacity and impersonal institutions are strong. | Cross doctrine, receiver profile, institutional scaffolding, and crisis exposure; measure where finality, shame, loyalty, and correction authority attach. |
| Concealed-causal-design signature | Constructors who rely on an undisclosed operational payload will predict or engineer recipient classifications and institutional effects better than their public semantic wrapper alone would permit. | Compare private plans, training, audience segmentation, institutional design, and pre-intervention predictions with public doctrine and later effects. |
| Privileged-vision escalation | Claimed exclusive access to a higher subject's true interest predicts jurisdictional expansion, dissent pathologization, interpreter insulation, and closed failure explanations. | Longitudinally code claims of privileged access, formal powers, correction routes, succession, material privilege, treatment of dissent, and responses to failure. |

Polygenic research requires special methodological discipline. Scores developed in one ancestry or institutional environment may not transport cleanly to another; population stratification, assortative mating, indirect genetic effects, and gene–environment correlation complicate interpretation. These are not reasons to exclude genetics from the theory. They determine the design standards required for a credible test.

The strongest evidence would be prospective and incremental:

```text
pre-registered dispositional or polygenic prediction
                    ↓
random assignment to matched and mismatched mythology
                    ↓
measured transportation, identification, retention, and behaviour
                    ↓
out-of-sample replication
                    ↓
predicted mediation through the relevant moral or motivational mechanism
```

### 38. Illustrative applications and hypotheses

The following applications show how the general framework can organize inquiry. They are not all established conclusions.

#### Balinese subak

The water-temple system illustrates reality-coupled mythology: sacred order, democratic association, ecological coordination, and observable feedback reinforce one another.

#### Producer and egalitarian guardian

The pair illustrates differential moral resonance, complementary roles, status conflict, and the possibility that a protective cultural immune system becomes autoimmune when it misclassifies sustaining structures as enemies.

#### Enlightenment and Counter-Enlightenment

One proposed interpretation treats property, contract, markets, impersonal law, and individual rights as an **individual-sovereignty technology suite** transmitted partly through Enlightenment mythology. That suite is a term proposed in this synthesis. Counter-Enlightenment mythology may redescribe those institutions as concealed domination and ordinary acceptance as **false consciousness**. The latter expression is borrowed from [Friedrich Engels's 1893 letter to Franz Mehring](https://www.marxists.org/archive/marx/works/1893/letters/93_07_14.htm), but its use here is deliberately not identical: Engels used it within historical materialism for an ideologue's unrecognized real motives, whereas this application treats it as a mythological device for invalidating ordinary acceptance of an institutional order.

This application generates historical and genetic hypotheses:

- that European populations underwent changes in individualist dispositions through gene–culture coevolution;
- that Enlightenment mythology resonated especially with those dispositions;
- that collectivist counter-mythologies resonated with a differently weighted part of the population;
- that institutional and reproductive consequences subsequently reinforced or altered those distributions.

These claims require historical, behavioural-genetic, genomic, and cross-cultural testing. Their place in the theory is generative: they specify what evidence would strengthen or weaken the account.

#### Freud and Bernays: unbraiding and rebraiding

[Sigmund Freud's introductory lectures on psychoanalysis](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/38219) present a structured inquiry into unconscious connections, dreams, symptoms, resistance, and repetition. At the level of the present analogy, psychoanalysis is an art of **untying**: it follows apparently disconnected strands, reconstructs how they became bound, and makes an inherited or symptomatic formation available for reinterpretation.

Freud's nephew [Edward Bernays](https://blogs.loc.gov/preservation/2022/11/housing-bernays-scrapbooks/) became a pioneer of public relations and described organized opinion formation in [*Propaganda*](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Propaganda). At the level of the analogy, propaganda is an art of **weaving**: it joins desire, prestige, authority, symbol, event, and public conduct so that a new association becomes culturally available and eventually ordinary.

This produces a memorable family symmetry:

```text
Freud: structured inquiry into an existing psychic formation
                         ↓
                  untying the braid

Bernays: structured organization of symbols and public associations
                         ↓
                  weaving a new braid
```

The symmetry is interpretive rather than a complete intellectual history. Freud did not define psychoanalysis as population-level mythological deprogramming, and Bernays's practice cannot be reduced to psychoanalytic theory. Their relationship makes tangible, however, that the ability to dissolve an installed configuration and the ability to install a successor are complementary technologies.

#### Critical Theory and cynical corrosion

[Max Horkheimer's “Traditional and Critical Theory”](https://doi.org/10.5840/zfs19376265) is an early canonical statement of Frankfurt School Critical Theory. Horkheimer's stated project was not contentless negation: he connected critique to empirical disciplines and to the positive aim of giving social life a rational form.

The mythology-construction framework nevertheless treats a particular **critical-theoretical operation** as an important polemical failure mode:

```text
real institution under unavoidable constraints
                    ↓
comparison with an unspecified or impossible ideal
                    ↓
every compromise reclassified as guilt or concealed domination
                    ↓
ordinary confidence becomes evidence of naïveté or false consciousness
                    ↓
incumbent mythology loses authority before a viable successor is exposed to test
```

In its strongest formulation, this is a tool for delegitimizing a real thing for not being an ideal thing that cannot exist. It works as corrosive acid on an inherited worldview: it need not know the positive destination in operational detail, because its immediate task is to dissolve the recipient's faith in the present braid. Dialectical or revolutionary replacement can then occur after the incumbent's symbolic defences have failed.

The official description of James Lindsay's *New Discourses Bullets*, episode
134, [“The Essence of Critical
Theory”](https://newdiscourses.com/2025/11/the-essence-of-critical-theory/),
states the adversarial formulation unusually plainly:

> At the center of Woke thinking is critical theory. So, what is it? In short,
> critical theory is a tool for delegitimizing a real thing for not being some
> kind of sociopolitical ideal thing that cannot even exist. The way it works is
> by holding the real thing up to the standards of an imaginary ideal rather
> than against the contingencies of reality and criticizing it with the aim of
> breaking people's faith in it, simply for existing in reality. It's a wicked
> tool almost always used to undermine something legitimate so that something
> illegitimate can replace it (dialectics).

This wording is quoted from the published episode description; it is not
presented here as a timestamped transcription of Lindsay's spoken audio. The
passage contains claims of different kinds. The comparison with an unrealizable
ideal and the attempt to break faith specify a proposed mechanism. **Wicked** is
Lindsay's moral judgment. **Almost always** is his strong empirical
generalization and requires comparative historical and textual evidence about
targets, feasible alternatives, disclosed successor arrangements, and actual
consequences.

Culture Mechanics retains the quoted passage as an adversarial source
formulation and treats its proposed operation as a candidate polemical failure
mode. It does not thereby adopt the quotation as the neutral definition of
Critical Theory or accept its frequency claim without evidence. Critique can
expose real domination, contradiction, deception, and avoidable suffering. The
corrosive operation must therefore be demonstrated through its standards of
comparison, treatment of constraints, concealment of alternatives, and
replacement effects rather than inferred merely from the label *Critical
Theory*. This is also not [Max
Horkheimer's](https://doi.org/10.5840/zfs19376265) definition of his own
programme.

##### Minogue's more differentiated sequence

Kenneth Minogue's [*Alien Powers: The Pure Theory of
Ideology*](https://api.pageplace.de/preview/DT0400.9781351321556_A31471797/preview-9781351321556_A31471797.pdf)
develops a longer chain than the Lindsay formulation. It can be reconstructed as:

```text
particular grievance
→ attributed to an underlying oppressive structure
→ acceptance of the practice becomes evidence of conditioning
→ reform is dismissed because “everything” must change
→ the successor is specified mainly as the absence of oppression
→ criticism and struggle guide action instead of an operational goal
→ dissent is treated as false consciousness rather than useful feedback
→ a vanguard must fabricate the successor and reshape consciousness
→ correction of the revolutionary project becomes revisionism
→ critique survives even when faith in the positive utopia collapses
```

This is a Culture Mechanics reconstruction assembled from Minogue's argument, not
a list or diagram printed by him. Its transitions nevertheless track distinct
passages in the book. A local grievance becomes a functional account of the whole
structure; criticism is permitted to remain negative because “the only
improvement of anything is the improvement of everything”; and the destination
becomes a “blank-cheque utopia” whose concrete specification would expose it to
comparison, rejection, compromise, and political bargaining.

> **In this reconstruction, the ideological project is metaphysically maximal,
> institutionally underspecified, and operationally negative.**

The result is a characteristic operational inversion:

- the promised terminus is morally and metaphysically maximal but
  institutionally unresolved;
- the practices of grievance discovery, structural attribution, suspicion,
  delegitimation, and mobilization are comparatively concrete;
- action therefore navigates by contradictions in the incumbent order rather
  than by a testable model of the destination;
- after victory, unavoidable fabrication and subject formation fall to a
  powerful and corruptible vanguard;
- criticism of that problem tends toward reformism or revisionism and can
  therefore be classified as defection.

For Minogue this also disables politics as a correction process. Politics treats
citizens as free contributors to an inherently contestable activity in which
legitimate disagreement and debate may improve judgment. His pure model of
ideology instead begins from enslavement, attributes ordinary understanding to
the experience of domination, teaches the ideological truth, and seeks to disable
opposition. Counterjudgment can thus be processed as evidence of the very
conditioning at issue rather than as information about the revolutionary
project.

The 2006 introduction supplies the decisive post-utopian transition. Minogue
argues that after the positive image of achieved perfection lost credibility,
ideology “fell back” on the skepticism and suspicion that had always been its
first persuasive move. In Culture Mechanics terms, this is direct source support
for a possible **negative-operational displacement** pathway: the positive telos
weakens while repeatable negative practices retain identity, institutional use,
and mobilizing power.

**Minogue's two terminal judgments must remain distinct.** In the first argument,
the ideological terminus abolishes the distinctions among agent, world, desire,
risk, frustration, essence, and existence that make human practice possible.
Minogue concludes that pronouncing civilization rotten becomes hatred of any
possible human life and calls the proposal “the cosmic equivalent of a **suicide
pact**.” In the second argument, ideology extends domination until meaningfulness
itself becomes domination; because no human world without meaning is imaginable,
Minogue concludes that the metaphysical attempt to overthrow domination is “the
**attempt to destroy humanity**.”

The convenient compression *ideology is a suicide pact to destroy humanity* is
therefore not Minogue's exact single-sentence formulation. The two expressions
are his attributed terminal judgments reached through related but different
arguments. Culture Mechanics retains them without treating them as definitions,
evidence of conscious suicidal or exterminatory intent, proof of a direct
Marx-to-Edelman genealogy, or sufficient evidence of a Ragnarök trajectory.
Minogue himself also distinguishes the pure theory from actual political
movements, which must respond to what people want and commonly develop
revisionist tendencies.

[Helen Pluckrose and James A. Lindsay's *Cynical Theories*](https://www.ipgbook.com/cynical-theories-products-9781634312028.php) is relevant prior art because the authors trace what they regard as a development from postmodern theory into activist orthodoxies that threaten science, reason, liberal ethics, and open debate. The **acid** image is not presented as their phrase. Here it names the more general mythology-construction mechanism by which habitual suspicion can dissolve one governing worldview without yet earning confidence in another.

#### Critique–construction asymmetry and negative-operational displacement

The **critique–construction asymmetry hypothesis** does not claim that Marxist,
communist, post-Marxist, or broader revolutionary-left programmes lacked positive
ends. Emancipation, equality, solidarity, rational social organization, and human
flourishing are genuine positive commitments within different parts of those
traditions. The narrower hypothesis concerns the relative development of their
operational machinery:

> **grievance discovery, delegitimation, disarticulation, mobilization, and
> incumbent unbraiding can become more concrete, repeatable, institutionally
> rewarded, and portable than successor construction, maintenance, correction,
> succession, and stopping.**

The proposed circuit is:

```text
positive emancipatory promise
              ↓
diagnosis of the incumbent as domination
              ↓
specialized practices of disclosure, suspicion, and negation
              ↓
successor order remains comparatively abstract or deferred
              ↓
each settlement is reclassified as reproduction, recuperation, or concealed rule
              ↓
renewed critique and mobilization
```

**Negative-operational displacement** names a possible subordinate pathway within
that circuit. It occurs when the programme's concrete negative operations outlive
or displace its comparatively underspecified positive telos. The operations do
not need to be anyone's secret plan. They can persist because they produce usable
research questions, recognizable roles, publication and status opportunities,
coalitional energy, enemies, and immediately legible interventions, while the
positive task remains constrained by trade-offs and answerable to consequences.

The existing finite-versus-renewable-demand distinction supplies a test. A
grievance attached to a finite operational demand can terminate when an observable
condition is met. A grievance becomes renewable when fulfilment is redescribed as
further evidence of hidden domination, the object is replaced without a stable
completion rule, or the identity and rewards of the programme depend upon
continuing negation. This does not make grievance false, criticism pathological,
or completion morally obligatory. New evidence can reveal an incomplete remedy;
the question is whether the revision remains answerable to independent
observations and declared stopping conditions.

Kenneth Minogue's *Alien Powers* is an important adversarial source lead for this
hypothesis. He describes ideology as continuous criticism, as suspicion of
apparently free institutions, and as a politics-disabling claim that dominated
people cannot contribute equal judgment. His account is not adopted here as a
neutral history of Marxism or Critical Theory. The [reconstructed
sequence](#minogues-more-differentiated-sequence) and his two terminal judgments
are retained as attributed source claims, not converted into the mechanism or
accepted as proof of intent. Culture Mechanics instead treats the persistence of
critical operations, the abstraction of the successor, and the response to
fulfilled demands as separately testable claims.

The mechanism is not left-specific and does not define Critical Theory. Religious
purification, nationalist revanchism, anti-corruption campaigns, market
fundamentalism, and organizational reform can exhibit the same asymmetry.
Criticism may also be indispensable to life-serving repair. Classification
therefore requires comparative evidence about operational detail, achieved
reforms, viable replacement, maintenance, correction, and stopping--not a
programme label or hostile rhetoric alone. The possible relation to the
death-drive pole is developed in the [drive-direction
analysis](#19-psychologically-downhill-and-lacanian-death-drive-mythologies);
asymmetry can increase that risk but does not establish it.

A concrete critique programme may also instantiate **un-reason** when its
critical machinery recursively converts counterevidence, successful reform,
internal dissent, or failed consequence into confirmation of the protected
account. Critique–construction asymmetry does not establish that further
mechanism: un-reason requires evidence that the programme's reasoning rules and
defeat conditions themselves have become asymmetrical and self-immunizing.

#### Constrained and unconstrained visions

[Thomas Sowell's *A Conflict of Visions*](https://www.hachettebookgroup.com/titles/thomas-sowell/a-conflict-of-visions/9780465002054/) distinguishes visions that emphasize permanent human limitation and trade-offs from visions that place greater confidence in reasoned social improvement. Mythology construction treats such visions as causal-worldview priors. The same proposal may be rejected before its values are considered because it presupposes an incompatible account of human nature, knowledge, power, and unintended consequence.

#### Democracy export

This application distinguishes exporting visible institutions from constructing the lifted solidarity, impersonal trust, and mythology of legitimate opposition that allow those institutions to reproduce themselves.

#### Marriage rules and social radius

This application tests whether rules governing close-kin marriage can jointly change reproductive structure, alliance networks, trust radius, institutional form, and the mythologies capable of travelling through the population.

#### Cultural interoperability breakdown (Babelization)

This application treats intergroup semantic decoupling as a loss of effective
shared lifted-solidarity surface. Local groups can retain strong mythology and
full local slot occupancy while the society falls below its cultural-
interoperability floor (anti-Babel floor) and loses a common protocol for
formulating and pursuing goals. Its residual interoperability scale can range
from rival mass domains through subcultures and very small groups to the
individual limiting case. Subjective loneliness is one possible downstream
sensor when even small-group recognition and translation fail, not a definition
or sufficient diagnostic of breakdown.

#### Destructive culture

This application begins with observable damage, reconstructs the mythology and PCP sequence that made it intelligible, and then tests causal attribution before assigning responsibility.

### 39. Diagnostic questions

#### Definition and purpose

- What exactly is the mythology intended to construct?
- Which real coordination problem does it address?
- Which outcomes would distinguish success from mere replication?
- Whose flourishing and whose costs are included?

#### Endogenous trajectory and purposive intervention

- Is the process being described endogenous mythology formation, purposive
  mythology construction, or an interaction between them?
- What is the evidenced baseline trajectory if the proposed intervention does not
  succeed?
- What destination \(D^*\) has been chosen, by whom, for whom, and under which
  jurisdiction?
- Why is mythology the relevant intervention surface, and which non-mythological
  systems, incentives, habits, or material conditions must change with it?
- How could receivers, incumbent institutions, rivals, unintended consequences, or
  reality redirect the intended successor?
- Which observations would distinguish progress toward \(D^*\) from improved
  replication of the mythology itself?

#### Historical genesis and authorization

- Which structural, institutional, practical, demographic, or dispositional
  changes preceded the explicit mythology?
- Is the mythology initially an articulation of a receiver–order mismatch, an
  imported construction, or both?
- Which need or aspiration gives the formulation authority, and does that fit
  survive criticism of its factual claims?
- What functional remainder is left when the explicit doctrine is removed?
- When did the formulation become an upstream cause through sacralization,
  institution, selection, and repeated transmission?
- Does the account distinguish witness observation, causal explanation,
  normative judgment, and intervention?

#### Doctrinal progeny and privileged authority

- What descendants did the doctrine actually produce, and what does their
  distribution show that isolated preferred examples conceal?
- For each descendant, is the relation entailment, authorization or affordance,
  selection, concealed causal design, appropriation or inversion, or a mixture?
- Are apparently repeated cases independent, or do they share an institutional
  template, sponsor, geopolitical pressure, or common founder?
- Which explicit propositions address the CPU, and which sacred classifications,
  attachments, sanctions, interpreters, and institutions supply the operational
  payload?
- Was that payload disclosed? What did the constructor know, foresee, or learn
  after implementation?
- Under which receiver architectures does the doctrine remain a corrigible lifted
  generator, undergo first-order recapture, or fail to take hold?
- Who claims privileged access to the true interest of an agentified higher
  subject, and how is that access tested?
- By what step is a knowledge claim converted into jurisdiction over other
  persons?
- Can those persons refuse, inspect, contest, replace, and leave the interpreter
  while retaining ordinary standing?
- Does failure correct the doctrine and its representatives, or confirm that
  opponents are blind and more control is required?

#### Resonance

- Which species-typical mechanisms does the mythology activate?
- Which dispositional configuration does it fit?
- Which moral taste buds and motives receive emphasis?
- Which relationships, role-contexts, institutions, practices, legal orders, or
  shared futures do recipients already experience as relevant?
- What does the mythology claim will strengthen, protect, corrupt, betray, or
  destroy them, and how credible is that causal account?
- Which structures and costs disappear from the represented effect model?
- Do the mythology's actual effects strengthen one dimension or constituency by
  weakening another, and how are conflicts, standing, and jurisdiction resolved?
- Which structures are being foregrounded, and which are being made relevant
  through identification or mirroring?
- Is the observed change only relevance activation, or has later behaviour shown
  durable relevance reprogramming?
- Which causal worldview does it assume?
- Does it offer recognition or require identity humiliation?
- Are several compatible variants required?

#### Grammar and PCP

- What are the topoi and causal plot?
- Who receives agency, intention, responsibility, and victimhood?
- Which sacred objects and master signifiers organize the field?
- What identity and concrete role are offered?
- What does the mythology make perceptible?
- Which context does it supply?
- What does it thereby permit or require?
- Against what anticipated recipient knowledge, symbols, lens, and context was
  the message compressed?
- What complete factual, causal, moral, and authorization model did recipients
  predictably and actually reconstruct?
- What power is requested, over whom or what, within which jurisdiction and
  duration, with what oversight, revocability, termination rule, and epistemic
  spillover?
- Which rival causal story fits the same observations?

#### Braiding, apologetics, polemics, and replacement

- Which strands form the incumbent rope of logic?
- Which programming packages are compatible constituents of the one incumbent
  weave, and where do rival packages contradict its load-bearing classifications?
- Does the analysis mistakenly rank parallel governing programs instead of testing
  compatible composition, rejection, or unbraid/rebraid replacement?
- Which cross-bindings carry its governing authority?
- Which strands are false or destructive, and which preserve recoverable knowledge or social goods?
- Does its apologetics answer strong objections and repair error, or reinterpret every objection as hostility?
- What exactly must polemics unbraid before the successor can become intelligible?
- Does the polemic compare the incumbent with a feasible alternative or an undefined perfection?
- What bridge prevents the liminal interval from becoming generalized cynicism, anomie, or third-party capture?
- Which situated scripts merely need repair, which require replacement, and which
  failures implicate the governing cultural braid itself?
- Which scripts are culturally load-bearing or mutually dependent, and could
  their failure create a cross-script cascade?
- Are successor roles, scripts, rituals, interfaces, institutions, skills, and
  material supports ready before the incumbent loses authority?
- Which incumbent strands will survive, and what operative meaning will their new
  bindings give them in the successor weave?
- Is the successor a first-order or lifted full capture: do participants share its
  concrete answers or the rules that generate, select, apply, and revise them?
- How mobilizationally absolute is the successor, and is that absoluteness being
  mistaken for its order level, field strength, jurisdiction, or corrigibility?
- Is an apparent revolutionary overshoot strategic, sincerely experienced, or
  selected among competing variants?
- What achieved jurisdiction or protection is the overshoot supposed to secure,
  and what evidence would show that a more accurate formulation could have done so?
- Will later institutions preserve the achievement while correcting the excess,
  or install the excess as another closed order?
- Who bears the transition costs and responsibility for the permissions created by replacement?

#### Actors, slots, and authority

- Which apologetic and polemical slots does the solidarity field create?
- Who currently occupies each slot: a person, office, institution, or informal network?
- Is the visible human speaking personally or as the representative of a persistent institutional actor?
- Which dispositions, motives, abilities, and biographical features drew the actor toward the role?
- How did the relevant group or institution recognize, ratify, appoint, or reject the claimant?
- Would the same words carry the same force from someone outside the recognized slot?
- Is authority grounded in lineage, charisma, ritual, credential, procedure, reputation, office, or some combination?
- Was the node recognized organically, appointed internally, externally amplified,
  deliberately cultivated, or selected through a dependency-shaped succession
  process?
- Which source, funder, evidence package, communication network, or appointment
  pathway is shared by apparently independent authorities?
- How did the message change as it passed through expert, institutional, regional,
  pastoral, and peer channels?
- Can the actor distinguish defence of the mythology's purpose from defence of personal or organizational survival?
- What happens when the actor fails, dies, retires, defects, or loses legitimacy?
- Can succession preserve authority without suppressing correction or recreating personal rule?
- Does the analysis mistake a bounded representative institution for the universal
  lifted meta-order, or allow an office to spill beyond its allocated jurisdiction?

#### Psychological gradient

- What immediate reward makes entry attractive?
- Which fantasy teaches the participant what to desire and what they are for the
  Other; which culturally available objects and roles does the mythology supply
  to that fantasy?
- Is desire given a finite operational demand with a real satisfaction condition,
  or converted into an impossible or renewable demand?
- If the stated demand is fulfilled, is completion allowed to terminate it, or
  does the circuit generate disappointment, a successor object, intensified
  accusation, or a new enemy?
- Are the promised positive goods specified in practices, institutions,
  maintenance duties, correction routes, succession arrangements, and stopping
  conditions commensurate with the programme's critical machinery?
- After a remedy succeeds, does mobilization subside, move to an independently
  evidenced remainder, or preserve itself by making satisfaction impossible?
- Does participation produce capability or mainly recognition?
- Does accepting the compressed explanation produce relief from uncertainty,
  contradiction, exposure, or responsibility; and does that relief reduce
  checking?
- Is anger transformed into protection and investigation or consumed through catharsis?
- Does the mythology educate taste or preserve immaturity?
- Does each repetition make exit more difficult?

#### Drive direction and sublimation

- Which raw drives are being released, repressed, or sublimated?
- Which cultural sublimation architecture selects, teaches, rewards, embodies,
  and transmits the proposed transformation?
- What is the generator kernel, what actuates its binding directions, and which
  practice turns them into durable capacity?
- Is duty being used as rule-bound self-command, as the continuing floor beneath
  richer capability, as mere obedience to an interpreter, or as proof that
  sacrifice is virtuous?
- Which symbolic carriers activate its installed mappings, and what does the
  local bigOther classify when they are encountered?
- Is a rival mythology producing temporary contrary activation, repeated
  reinforcement, or durable reprogramming of the mythological lens?
- Does felt relevance track the architecture's independently evidenced
  generative centrality, or is inherited capability being consumed without
  maintenance or replacement?
- What becomes easier after one year of participation?
- What skills, relationships, institutions, and inheritances are created?
- What is consumed without replacement?
- Whom or what does the resulting capability serve: the declared user, an
  effective beneficiary, a competitive dynamic, programme continuation, a
  life-supporting form, or a self-consuming trajectory?
- Do persons remain principals who can redirect and stop the tool or institution,
  or are their conduct and formation increasingly reorganized around serving its
  continuation and demands?
- Does difficulty serve life, or has sacrifice itself become a death-driven ritual?

#### Affective return and counterculture

- Which concrete capability, relationship, possible self, or successor horizon
  was lost or foreclosed, and what evidence connects the alleged obstacle to that
  loss?
- Does grief disclose a finite loss, and does anger remain discriminating enough
  to support inquiry, proportionate protection, repair, and eventual release?
- Is the alleged foreclosed future a plausible developmental path with a
  supported causal account, or an idealized counterfactual being converted into
  grievance? Did assistance scaffold capability or substitute for its formation?
- Does shame distinguish continuing personal standing from specific conduct, or
  does it collapse into humiliation, global worthlessness, or permanent stigma?
- Has the provider become visible as another limited subject with costs and
  legitimate claims, or is parent, state, Society, History, or Justice still
  imagined as an omnipotent supplier?
- Is a deservingness claim grounded in contribution, bounded dependency,
  retained readiness, or another disclosed basis, and can it terminate when the
  basis disappears?
- Does the successor offer intrinsically rewarding practice, fellowship,
  apprenticeship, humour, play, visible mastery, repair, and honourable return,
  or only harder rules and moral accusation?
- Does support make the cost of independent judgment survivable while leaving the
  judgment genuinely open, or does belonging depend upon reaching the custodian's
  answer?
- Are civilizational and wild-but-sublimated modes both available, and do their
  immediate rewards form capability, reciprocity, correction, maintenance, or
  succession rather than excitement or conformity alone?
- Can the Child at Play remain an immanent invitation to present vitality while
  concrete children, nonparents, dependants, and different life courses retain
  equal standing?
- Does Guardian trust protect the player and playfield without becoming an
  internal brake, an unreviewable protector, or a claim to permanent custody?
- Who can actually enter the playfield, and which risks or costs are displaced
  onto excluded persons, future participants, or the surrounding world?
- Can the counterculture win and then stop fighting, or must Piper, Bandit, enemy,
  grievance, or fallen carrier remain constitutive of its identity?

#### Humanity, exemplars, and reflective audit

- Does *Humanity* mean equal standing instantiated in every concrete person, an
  open horizon of plural human possibility, or an agentified collective subject?
- Does plurality serve concrete persons and viable forms of life, or has a
  selected pattern of differences become the protected principal?
- Who controls the categories, metric, baseline, deficit, remedy, exception, and
  completion rule, and would alternative specifications change the result?
- What happens to the mixed, unclassified, category-refusing,
  nonrepresentative, or pattern-conflicting person?
- Does the destination protect infants, dependants, dissenters, unproductive
  persons, defeated rivals, and those who cannot approximate its exemplars?
- Which *Leuchttürme* and near exemplars make possible development visible?
- Are the disciplines, institutions, dependencies, failures, and costs behind
  exemplary achievement represented, or only the prestige surface?
- Does excellence in one domain silently expand into moral, epistemic, or
  political authority in another?
- Can recipients distinguish when reason is acting as press secretary,
  interpreter, or auditor?
- What observation would change the generator, its application, or its authorized
  interpretation?
- Does reflective criticism produce an actual finality cut, or only a more
  elaborate defence of the same bigOther verdict?

#### Embodiment

- Which practices, rituals, calendars, offices, and rewards tell the same story?
- Do admired exemplars perform the difficult work?
- Does the budget embody the stated sacred values?
- What happens during crisis, failure, and succession?

#### Social scale

- At what radius must cooperation operate?
- Does the mythology bridge groups or intensify one local cluster?
- Can political or institutional losers remain members?
- Are central terms mutually intelligible across subcultures?
- Is synchronization calibrated to preserve independent judgment?
- Does the proposal increase the effective thickness and span of shared lifted
  solidarity, or contribute to cultural interoperability breakdown
  (Babelization)?
- Which installed meta-rules generate the relevant first-order answers, and which
  of them allocate, limit, coordinate, and correct jurisdictions?

#### Reality and epistemic constitution

- What evidence could reduce confidence in the mythology?
- Who records failed predictions and adverse consequences?
- Can authorities admit error without loss of their entire identity?
- Are correction routes causally independent?
- Can a recipient leave with dignity?
- Does failure cause revision or demands for greater commitment?

#### Adversarial defence and accountability

- Which dark-art techniques could capture or invert the mythology?
- Could a trusted guardian become an authenticated delivery channel through
  capture of a trusted opinion leader or of the institution or channel they
  represent, including sentinel inversion?
- Are provenance, material relationships, message construction, and source
  dependence visible at every stage of the cascade?
- Can recipients recognize the downhill honey-trap signature?
- Does a visible *Wie* of goodness, expertise, care, or legitimacy substitute for
  the actual *Was*--the function, conduct, and consequences the cue is supposed to
  identify?
- Is there a superior constructive role, not merely a debunking message?
- Are recovery routes available?
- Are sanctions tied to demonstrable conduct, causal responsibility, and due process?
- Could the label “dark artist” itself become an enemy-construction trick?

## Terminological provenance and boundaries

This section separates three kinds of language:

1. **retained:** used substantially in its cited source sense;
2. **borrowed and extended:** recognizably derived from a cited author, but given a broader, narrower, or different operational role here;
3. **proposed here:** part of the present synthesis and not attributed to an external author.

A citation under “retained” supports the named concept, not every inference later made from it. A concept under “borrowed and extended” must not be quoted back to its originator as though the adapted definition were theirs.

### 40. Established concepts retained substantially in their source sense

- **Practical culture implementation through leadership behaviour, systems,
  symbols, mythologies, habits, organizational arrangements, and repeated
  experience--Ian Macdonald, Catherine Burke, and Karl Stewart:** [*Systems
  Leadership: Creating Positive
  Organisations*](https://www.routledge.com/Systems-Leadership-Creating-Positive-Organisations/MacDonald-Burke-Stewart/p/book/9781138036574).
  Culture Mechanics retains this as its definitive practical method and judges it
  approximately 99% correct. Translation into **bigOther** architecture and
  extension beyond the organizational and workplace focus belong to the present
  framework.
- **Mechanical and organic solidarity — Émile Durkheim:** [*De la division du travail social*](https://classiques.uqam.ca/classiques/Durkheim_emile/division_du_travail/division_travail.html).
- **Collective representations, ritual, and collective effervescence — Émile Durkheim:** [*The Elementary Forms of the Religious Life*](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/41360).
- **Unconscious social genesis, aspirational authority, political sacralization,
  and structural change — Émile Durkheim:** [“Les principes de 1789 et la
  sociologie”](https://education.persee.fr/doc/revin_1775-6014_1890_num_19_1_9561_t1_0450_0000_1)
  (1890), translated by Mark Traugott in [*Émile Durkheim on Morality and
  Society*](https://press.uchicago.edu/ucp/books/book/chicago/E/bo3627098.html),
  pp. 34–42. Durkheim supplies the observations and his historical hypothesis; the
  five-phase sequence, receiver–order mismatch, aspirational authorization,
  functional-remainder test, and revolutionary-overshoot hypothesis are project
  formulations.
- **Judging doctrines by what they produce and by “the spirit of the doctrines to
  which they give birth” — Émile Durkheim:** [“Individualism and the
  Intellectuals”](https://classiques.uqam.ca/classiques/Durkheim_emile/sc_soc_et_action/texte_3_10/individualisme.html)
  (1898), translated by Mark Traugott in [*Émile Durkheim on Morality and
  Society*](https://platypus1917.org/wp-content/uploads/The-Heritage-of-Sociology-Emile-Durkheim-On-Morality-and-Society_-Selected-Writings-University-of-Chicago-Press-1973.pdf),
  pp. 43–57. Durkheim supplies the maxim and his Kant–Fichte, Kant–Hegel–Marx,
  Rousseau, and French-Revolution examples. The doctrinal-progeny test, offspring
  profile, generative spirit, five-relation taxonomy, concealed-causal-design
  category, recipient-architecture bifurcation, and first-order-recapture
  hypothesis are project developments.
- **Moral Foundations Theory — Jonathan Haidt, Craig Joseph, Jesse Graham, and collaborators:** [Moral Foundations Theory publications](https://moralfoundations.org/publications/).
- **Biology as a first draft elaborated by cultural learning — Jonathan Haidt:** [*The Righteous Mind*](https://www.penguinrandomhouse.com/books/73535/the-righteous-mind-by-jonathan-haidt/).
- **Gene–culture coevolution joined to the possibility of fast historical
  evolution — Jonathan Haidt:** [chapter 9 of *The Righteous
  Mind*](https://righteousmind.com/wp-content/uploads/2012/08/RighteousMind.Chapter-9.pdf).
  Haidt supplies the project's immediate synthesis and temporal bridge, not the
  quantitative origin of gene–culture theory or the project's Europe-specific
  extension.
- **Narrative transportation — Melanie Green and Timothy Brock:** [“The Role of Transportation in the Persuasiveness of Public Narratives”](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11079236/).
- **Framing — Robert Entman:** [“Framing: Toward Clarification of a Fractured Paradigm”](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1460-2466.1993.tb01304.x).
- **Causal stories — Deborah Stone:** [“Causal Stories and the Formation of Policy Agendas”](https://www.psqonline.org/article.cfm?IDArticle=12446).
- **Defensive and offensive apologetics — John G. Stackhouse:** [“Defining, Directing, and Defending Apologetics”](https://academic.oup.com/book/12859/chapter/163145885).
- **Apologetics and polemics as distinguishable theological functions — Friedrich Schleiermacher:** [*Brief Outline of the Study of Theology*](https://openlibrary.org/books/OL7182960M/Brief_outline_of_the_study_of_theology).
- **Psychoanalytic inquiry into unconscious formations, resistance, and symptoms — Sigmund Freud:** [*A General Introduction to Psychoanalysis*](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/38219).
- **Organized public-opinion formation and propaganda — Edward Bernays:** [*Propaganda*](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Propaganda).
- **Memetic engineering as a speculative cultural-control aspiration — James
  Gardner:** [“Memetic
  Engineering”](https://www.wired.com/1996/05/memetic/) (1996). The essay supplies
  the historical phrase and a conjectural image of deliberately altering
  interacting cultural replicators; it does not establish a working science,
  precise cultural control, or Culture Mechanics' ontology.
- **Critical Theory — Max Horkheimer:** [“Traditional and Critical Theory”](https://doi.org/10.5840/zfs19376265).
- **Critique of postmodern and activist theories — Helen Pluckrose and James A. Lindsay:** [*Cynical Theories*](https://www.ipgbook.com/cynical-theories-products-9781634312028.php).
- **Critical Theory as delegitimation against an unrealizable ideal — James
  Lindsay:** the official description of *New Discourses Bullets*, episode 134,
  [“The Essence of Critical
  Theory”](https://newdiscourses.com/2025/11/the-essence-of-critical-theory/)
  supplies the quoted adversarial formulation. It is treated as a source claim,
  not Horkheimer's definition or a frequency finding accepted without evidence.
- **Ideology as structuralized grievance, negative terminus, politics-disabling
  revelation, and post-utopian suspicion — Kenneth Minogue:** [*Alien Powers:
  The Pure Theory of
  Ideology*](https://api.pageplace.de/preview/DT0400.9781351321556_A31471797/preview-9781351321556_A31471797.pdf).
  The multi-step sequence is a Culture Mechanics reconstruction of Minogue's
  argument. “Suicide pact” and “attempt to destroy humanity” are his distinct
  terminal judgments, not one combined quotation or evidence of actor intent.
- **Nature as a constituency unable to dismiss its claimed representative and
  the proposed red-to-green transition — David Horowitz:** [“From Red to Green,” in *Deconstructing the Left: From Vietnam to the Persian Gulf*](https://books.google.com/books/about/Deconstructing_the_Left.html?id=LQUEAQAAIAAJ).
  Horowitz supplies a situated polemical interpretation and the silent-
  constituency image. He does not supply the project's complete
  silent-principal mechanism, establish the genealogy, prove strategic intent, or
  classify environmentalism as a whole.
- **Explicit ahuman anti-continuation — Patricia MacCormack:** [*The Ahuman
  Manifesto*](https://www.bloomsbury.com/uk/ahuman-manifesto-9781350081093/) and
  its publisher-authorized author preview supply the explicit call to end the
  human conceptually and as a species through cessation of reproduction while
  caring for existing persons. This establishes her stated programme, not violent
  extermination, institutional capability, or agreement by adjacent fields.
- **Reproductive futurism, the symbolic Child, queer negativity, and death-drive
  — Lee Edelman:** [*No Future: Queer Theory and the Death
  Drive*](https://web.english.upenn.edu/~cavitch/pdf-library/Edelman_No_Future.pdf).
  The Child is a political-symbolic figure rather than actual children, and the
  source does not define homosexuality, queer people, gender nonconformity, or
  queer theory generally or propose literal human extinction.
- **Affirmative posthuman displacement of traditional Humanist unity — Rosi
  Braidotti:** the official [Wiley description of *The
  Posthuman*](https://www.wiley-vch.de/en/areas-interest/humanities-social-sciences/the-posthuman-978-0-7456-4157-7)
  joins posthuman displacement to multiple identities, sustainability,
  empowerment, social bonding, community-building, and neo-humanism. This is a
  publisher-attested source bridge rather than full-text evidence for every
  detail, and it establishes neither anti-futurity, extinctionism, death-drive,
  nor destructive service direction.
- **Active gene–environment correlation — Robert Plomin, John DeFries, and John Loehlin:** [“Genotype-Environment Interaction and Correlation in the Analysis of Human Behavior”](https://doi.org/10.1037/0033-2909.84.2.309).
- **Active niche selection and construction — Sandra Scarr and Kathleen McCartney:** [“How People Make Their Own Environments”](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/6683622/).
- **Polygenic inheritance and scores in behavioural genetics — Robert Plomin:** [*Blueprint*](https://mitpress.mit.edu/9780262537988/blueprint/).
- **Increasing heritability of cognitive ability across development — Daniel Briley and Elliot Tucker-Drob:** [“Explaining the Increasing Heritability of Cognitive Ability across Development”](https://doi.org/10.1177/0956797613478618).
- **Quantitative cultural evolution and gene–culture coevolution — L. Luca Cavalli-Sforza and Marcus Feldman, later Robert Boyd, Peter Richerson, Joseph Henrich, and other dual-inheritance theorists:** [Marcus Feldman's account of the programme's origins](https://www.santafe.edu/people/profile/marc-feldman) and [“Gene-Culture Coevolution in the Age of Genomics”](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3024025/).
- **Kinship intensity and psychological variation — Jonathan Schulz, Duman Bahrami-Rad, Jonathan Beauchamp, and Joseph Henrich:** [“The Church, Intensive Kinship, and Global Psychological Variation”](https://doi.org/10.1126/science.aau5141).
- **Articulation and hegemony — Ernesto Laclau and Chantal Mouffe:** [*Hegemony and Socialist Strategy*](https://www.versobooks.com/en-gb/products/1158-hegemony-and-socialist-strategy).
- **Folk devil — Stanley Cohen:** [*Folk Devils and Moral Panics*](https://www.routledge.com/Folk-Devils-and-Moral-Panics-1st-Edition/Cohen/p/book/9781138834743).
- **Organizational culture embedding — Edgar Schein:** [*Organizational Culture and Leadership*](https://www.wiley-vch.de/de/?isbn=978-1-119-21204-1&option=com_eshop&view=product).
- **Self-Determination Theory — Edward Deci and Richard Ryan:** [“The ‘What’ and ‘Why’ of Goal Pursuits”](https://doi.org/10.1207/S15327965PLI1104_01).
- **Psychological inoculation — William McGuire:** [“Resistance to Persuasion Conferred by Active and Passive Prior Refutation”](https://doi.org/10.1037/h0048344). **Technique-based misinformation inoculation — Jon Roozenbeek, Sander van der Linden, Beth Goldberg, Steve Rathje, and Stephan Lewandowsky:** [“Psychological Inoculation Improves Resilience against Misinformation on Social Media”](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9401631/).
- **Perspective-getting through nonjudgmental narrative exchange — Joshua Kalla and David Broockman:** [“Reducing Exclusionary Attitudes through Interpersonal Conversation”](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0003055419000923).
- **“A Better Story” — Alliance for Responsible Citizenship (ARC):** ARC's [“A
  Better Story” research
  category](https://www.arc-conference.com/research-papers?category=A+Better+Story)
  supplies the affirmative narrative umbrella retained in section 33.3. The
  claim that a successor culture must also provide the Better Song, sharper
  joke, livelier festival, and exhilarating contest—and the associated
  vitalization, mythic-radiance, and agonistic-sublimation relations—is a Culture
  Mechanics extension, not an ARC method or a validation of the successor.
- **Three metamorphoses of the spirit — Friedrich Nietzsche:** [*Thus Spoke
  Zarathustra*, “The Three
  Metamorphoses”](https://www.gutenberg.org/files/1998/1998-h/1998-h.htm)
  supplies Camel, Lion, and Child, including the Child as new beginning, game,
  and affirmative “Yea.” The mapping to formation, unbraiding, rebraiding, the
  Child at Play, and the stopping condition is Culture Mechanics' interpretation.
- **Political immaturity as family grammar generalized into adult politics —
  Jordan B. Peterson:** his [conversation with Matt
  Walsh](https://podcasts.apple.com/bh/podcast/549-matt-walsh-on-making-movies-the-woke-right-winning/id1184022695?i=1000709452871)
  supplies the developmental, political, temporal, affective, Golden-Calf, and
  political-conclusion claims retained in section 33.3. The Lacanian mature and
  immature grammars, provider-subject transition, deservingness circuits, and
  application tests are project developments.
- **Addiction-recovery source family:** reinforcer pathology, motivational
  interviewing, episodic future thinking, Community Reinforcement Approach,
  contingency management, recovery capital and identity, relapse prevention,
  and AA/Twelve-Step Facilitation supply the distinct external mechanisms cited
  in section 33.3. No source supplies the complete affective-return sequence or
  identifies cultural adherence with addiction.
- **Balinese water-temple networks as complex adaptive systems — J. Stephen Lansing and James Kremer:** [“Emergent Properties of Balinese Water Temple Networks”](https://doi.org/10.1525/aa.1993.95.1.02a00050).
- **Hairworm alteration of cricket water-entry behaviour — Frédéric Thomas and colleagues:** [“Do Hairworms Manipulate the Water Seeking Behaviour of Their Terrestrial Hosts?”](https://doi.org/10.1046/j.1420-9101.2002.00410.x).
- **Constrained and unconstrained visions — Thomas Sowell:** [*A Conflict of Visions*](https://www.hachettebookgroup.com/titles/thomas-sowell/a-conflict-of-visions/9780465002054/).
- **The Grail question and technological service direction — Jonathan Pageau:**
  his [ARC 2026
  talk](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=yZUuKzDQSsI&t=250s) supplies the Grail and
  Lance as images of technological abundance and power, the question “Whom does
  the Grail serve?”, the Moloch-trap account of competitive escalation and
  sacrifice, and the concluding turn from what technology serves to what its
  builders serve. The decomposition into declared and effective beneficiaries,
  operative objectives, sacrificed substrates, mastery reversal, correction, and
  temporal trajectory is a Culture Mechanics audit, not Pageau's formal
  diagnostic. The accessible transcript is third-party rather than an official
  ARC publication.

### 41. Borrowed concepts deliberately extended here

The following terms retain a recognizable source lineage but receive a new functional role in this synthesis.

#### Mythology and mythological lens

**Status: borrowed and substantially extended.** The framework adapts terminology
from [Ian Macdonald, Catherine Burke, and Karl Stewart's *Systems Leadership:
Creating Positive
Organisations*](https://www.routledge.com/Systems-Leadership-Creating-Positive-Organisations/MacDonald-Burke-Stewart/p/book/9781138036574)
and joins it to Durkheimian collective representation, narrative theory, cultural
evolution, and the newly proposed culture-mechanics model. A mythology here is not
merely a sacred tale; it is a connected system of evaluated narrative meaning that
trains population-level classification and can be embodied and reinforced through
symbols, practices, systems, and institutions. Those implementation channels are
not thereby reduced to mythology. The full extended definition should not be
attributed to the Systems Leadership authors.

This extension of terminology and theoretical range is not a general correction of
the book's culture-implementation method. Culture Mechanics treats that practical
method as definitive and approximately 99% correct; departures must be named as
extensions or revisions rather than attributed back to Macdonald, Burke, and
Stewart.

#### Relevant structures and Wegner-derived hypotheses

**Status: borrowed and substantially extended.** Dushan Wegner's *Relevante
Strukturen* (2019; ISBN 9783739405636) supplies the relevant-structure vocabulary
and the source hypothesis that people experience support for a relevant structure
as good and its weakening as evil. Culture Mechanics retains that proposal as the
descriptive **relevant-structure appraisal hypothesis** and adds the
**relevant-structure resonance hypothesis** concerning differential mythology
adoption, retention, enactment, and transmission. The typed relational definition,
the separation of felt relevance, represented effect, actual multidimensional
effect, generative centrality, and normative standing, the integration with the
one mythological lens, and the proposed tests belong to the present synthesis.
They should not be
attributed to Wegner as his complete theory or treated as proof that perceived
support is actually beneficial or morally justified.

Wegner also supplies the originating emphases on *Veränderung*, *Spiegelung*,
what is placed “vorm Auge,” **“Ethik ist eine Waffe,”** and the **Vierschritt der
Kontrolle**. The named **change-indexed moral-mobilization**,
**mirrored-relevance**, and **foregrounded-relevance** hypotheses and the
**relevant-structure-weaponization schema** are the present synthesis's bounded,
testable translations. They preserve the source language while distinguishing
activation from reprogramming, moral appraisal from moral truth, and legitimate
reasoning about effects from manipulative selection or fabrication.

Jaap Hanekamp supplies the external ecological formulation **“Deterioration is a
moral evaluation of change”** in a user-supplied transcript of his [EpochTV
interview](https://www.theepochtimes.com/epochtv/rise-of-scientism-and-the-junk-climate-models-used-by-government-to-take-farmers-land-facts-matter-5652622).
The accessible programme page confirms the interview but does not reproduce the
complete transcript; the line is therefore preserved with that transcript
provenance. Hanekamp and William Briggs's peer-reviewed [critical-load
paper](https://doi.org/10.1177/15593258221075513) is a neighbouring source for
their ecological-endpoint and uncertainty critique, not the verified source of
this sentence. The controlled link to Wegner's appraisal hypothesis, the typed
sequence from change through deterioration to permission, and the cross-link to
metric custody are Culture Mechanics developments. They neither attribute
Hanekamp's application to Wegner nor establish Hanekamp's stronger model,
policy, motive, or scientism verdicts.

The subsequent Dutch worked audit uses source layers that must remain distinct.
The 2020 government-appointed advisory report supplies the bounded finding that
the national measurement and modelling system was broadly fit for purpose while
the then-current AERIUS Calculator was not fit for permit decisions at the
requested granularity. RIVM supplies both the 2023 scale-dependent uncertainty
